Controlling unfolding in type theory
Daniel Gratzer, Jonathan Sterling, Carlo Angiuli, Thierry Coquand, Lars Birkedal

Abstract
We present a novel mechanism for controlling the unfolding of definitions in dependent type theory. Traditionally, proof assistants let users specify whether each definition can or cannot be unfolded in the remainder of a development; unfolding definitions is often necessary in order to reason about them, but an excess of unfolding can result in brittle proofs and intractably large proof goals. In our system, definitions are by default not unfolded, but users can selectively unfold them in a local manner. We justify our mechanism by means of elaboration to a core type theory with extension types , a connective first introduced in the context of homotopy type theory. We prove a normalization theorem for our core calculus and have implemented our system in the cool tt proof assistant, providing both theoretical and practical evidence for it.



1
Introduction

In dependent type theory, terms are type checked modulo definitional equality,
a congruence generated by
\alpha
-,
\beta
-, and
\eta
-laws, as well
unfolding of definitions. Unfolding definitions is to some extent a
convenience that allows type checkers to silently discharge many proof
obligations,
e.g
.
a list of length
1+1
is without further annotation also a
list of length
2
. It is by no means the case, however, that we always want a
given definition to unfold:

•

Modularity
: Dependent types are famously sensitive to the smallest changes to
definitions, such as whether
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
recurs on its first or its second argument. If we plan to
change a definition in the future, it may be desirable to avoid exposing its implementation to the
type checker.

•

Usability
: Although unfolding has the potential to simplify proof states, it also has
the potential to dramatically complicate them, resulting in unreadable subgoals, error messages,

etc
.
A user may find that certain definitions are likely to be problematic in this way, and thus
opt not to unfold them.

Many proof assistants accordingly have implementation-level support for marking definitions

opaque
(unable to be unfolded), including Agda’s
abstract
keyword
[
36
]
and
Coq’s
Qed
command
[
38
]
.

But unfolding definitions is not merely a matter of convenience: to prove a property of a function,
we must unfold it. For example, if we make the definition of the
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
function opaque, then

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is indistinguishable from a variable of type
\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
and so
cannot be shown to be commutative, satisfy
1+1=2
,
etc
.

In practice, proof assistants resolve this contradiction by adopting an intermediate stance:
definitions are
transparent
(unfolded during type checking) by default, but users are given
some control over their unfolding. Coq provides conversion tactics (
cbv
,
simpl
,

etc
.
) for applying definitional equalities, each of which can be annotated with a list of
definitions to unfold; its
Opaque
and
Transparent
commands toggle the default
unfolding behavior of a transparent definition; and the
SSReflect
tactic language natively
supports a “
lock
ing” idiom for controlling when definitions unfold
[
17
]
.
Agda allows users to group multiple definitions into a single
abstract
block, inside of
which the definitions are transparent and outside of which they are opaque; this allows users to
define a function, prove all lemmas that depend on how the function was defined, and then
irreversibly make the function and lemmas opaque.

These mechanisms for controlling unfolding are more subtle than they may at first appear. In Agda,
definitions within
abstract
blocks are transparent to other definitions in the same block, but
opaque to the
types
of those definitions; without such a stipulation, those types may cease
to be well-formed when the earlier definition is made opaque. Furthermore,
abstract
blocks
are anti-modular, requiring users to anticipate all future lemmas about definitions in a
block—indeed, the Agda standard library
[
37
]
uses
abstract
exactly once at
the time of writing. Coq’s conversion tactics are more flexible than Agda’s
abstract

blocks, but being tactics, their behavior can be harder to predict. The
lock
idiom in

SSReflect
is more predictable because it creates opaque definitions, but comes in four
different variations to simplify its use in practice.

Contributions

We propose a novel mechanism for fine-grained control over the unfolding of definitions in dependent
type theory. We introduce language-level primitives for
controlled unfolding
that
are elaborated into a core calculus with
extension types

[
30
]
, a connective
first introduced in the context of homotopy type theory. We justify our elaboration algorithm by
establishing a normalization theorem (and hence the decidability of type checking and injectivity of
type constructors) for our core calculus, and we have implemented our system for controlled
unfolding in the experimental
cool
tt
proof assistant
[
29
]
.

Definitions in our framework are opaque by default, but can be selectively and locally unfolded as
if they were transparent. Our system is finer-grained and more modular than Agda’s
abstract

blocks: we need not collect all lemmas that unfold a given definition into a single block, making
our mechanism better suited to libraries. Our primitives have more predictable meaning and
performance than Coq’s unfolding tactics because they are implemented by straightforward elaboration
into the core calculus (via new types and declaration forms); we anticipate that this

type-theoretic
account of controlled unfolding will also provide a clear path toward
integrating our ideas with future language features.

Our core calculus is intensional Martin-Löf type theory extended with
proof-irrelevant proposition symbols
p
, dependent products
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,A

over those propositions, and extension types
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle p\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle p\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
whose elements
are the elements of
A
that are definitionally equal to
a
under the assumption that

p
is true. Extension types are similar to the path types

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{Path}}~A~a_{0}~a_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{Path}}~A~a_{0}~a_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{Path}}~A~a_{0}~a_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{Path}}~A~a_{0}~a_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
of cubical type theory

[
9
,
3
,
2
]
, which classify functions out of an
abstract interval
\mathbb{I}
that are definitionally equal to
a_{0}
and
a_{1}

when evaluated at the interval’s respective endpoints
0,1:\mathbb{I}
.

To justify our elaboration algorithm, we prove a
normalization
theorem
for our core calculus, characterizing its definitional equivalence classes of
types and terms and as a corollary establishing the decidability of type
checking. Our proof adapts and extends Sterling’s technique of synthetic Tait
computability (STC)
[
34
,
31
]
, which has
previously been used to establish parametricity for an ML-style module calculus

[
34
]
and normalization for cubical type theory

[
33
]
and multimodal type theory
[
18
]
.
Our proof is fully constructive, an improvement on the prior work of Sterling
and Angiuli
[
33
]
; we have also corrected an error in the
handling of universes in an earlier revision of Sterling’s doctoral
dissertation
[
31
]
.

Outline

In
Section

2
we introduce our controlled unfolding primitives by way of
examples, and in
Section

3
we walk through how these examples are elaborated
into our core language of dependent type theory with proposition symbols and extension types. In

Section

4
we present our elaboration algorithm, and in
Section

5
we discuss our
implementation of the above in the
cool
tt
proof assistant. In
Section

6
we
establish normalization and decidability of type checking for our core calculus. We conclude with a
discussion of related work in
Section

7
.

2
A surface language with controlled unfolding

We begin by describing an Agda-like surface language for a dependent type
theory with controlled unfolding. In
Section

4
we will give
precise meaning to this language by explaining how to elaborate it into our
core calculus; for now we proceed by example, introducing our new primitives
bit by bit. Our examples will concern the inductively defined natural numbers
and their addition function:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}

{\textsf{ze}}+n=n

{{\textsf{su}}~m}+n={\textsf{su}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}

2.1
A simple dependency: length-indexed vectors

In our language, definitions such as
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
are opaque by default—they are not unfolded
automatically. To illustrate the need to
selectively
unfold
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, consider the indexed
inductive type of length-indexed vectors with the following constructors:

{\textsf{vnil}}:{\textsf{vec}}~{\textsf{ze}}~A

{\textsf{vcons}}:A\to{\textsf{vec}}~n~A\to{\textsf{vec}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{su}}~n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{su}}~n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{su}}~n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{su}}~n$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A

Suppose we attempt to define the
append
operation on vectors by dependent pattern matching on
the first vector. Our goals would be as follows:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A\to{\textsf{vec}}~n~A\to{\textsf{vec}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A

{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus v=\hbox{\pagecolor{RegalBlue!10}$\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textsf{vec}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{ze}}+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{ze}}+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{ze}}+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{ze}}+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A$}

{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u\oplus v=\hbox{\pagecolor{RegalBlue!10}$\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textsf{vec}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{su}}~m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{su}}~m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{su}}~m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{su}}~m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A$}

As it stands, the goals above are in normal form and cannot be proved; however, we may indicate that
the definition of
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
should be unfolded within the definition of
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
by adding the
following top-level
unfolds
annotation:

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}~{\textbf{unfolds}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}}

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A\to{\textsf{vec}}~n~A\to{\textsf{vec}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A

With our new declaration, the goals simplify:

{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus v=\hbox{\pagecolor{RegalBlue!10}$\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}

{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u\oplus v=\hbox{\pagecolor{RegalBlue!10}$\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textsf{vec}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{su}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{su}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{su}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{su}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A$}

The first goal is solved with
v
itself; for the second goal, we begin by applying the

vcons
constructor:

{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u\oplus v={\textsf{vcons}}~a~\hbox{\pagecolor{RegalBlue!10}$\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textsf{vec}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A$}

The remaining goal is just our induction hypothesis
u\oplus v
. All in all, we have:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}~{\textbf{unfolds}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A\to{\textsf{vec}}~n~A\to{\textsf{vec}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A

{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus v=v

{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u\oplus v={\textsf{vcons}}~a~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}

2.2
Transitive unfolding

Now suppose we want to prove that
map
distributes over
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. In doing so we will
certainly need to unfold
map
, but it turns out this will not be enough:

{\textsf{map}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\textsf{vec}}~n~A\to{\textsf{vec}}~n~B

{\textsf{map}}~f~{\textsf{vnil}}={\textsf{vnil}}

{\textsf{map}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}={\textsf{vcons}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{map}}~f~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{map}}~f~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{map}}~f~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{map}}~f~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}

map-
\oplus

unfolds

map

{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\to{\textsf{map}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv{\textsf{map}}~f~u\oplus{\textsf{map}}~f~v

{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~f~{\textsf{vnil}}~v=\hbox{\pagecolor{RegalBlue!10}$\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textsf{map}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus{\textsf{map}}~f~v$}

{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}~v

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textsf{map}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u}\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u}\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u}\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u}\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv{\textsf{vcons}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{map}}~f~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{map}}~f~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{map}}~f~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{map}}~f~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}\oplus{\textsf{map}}~f~v

To make further progress we must also unfold
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
:

{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~{\textbf{unfolds}}~{\textsf{map}};{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}}

{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\to{\textsf{map}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv{\textsf{map}}~f~u\oplus{\textsf{map}}~f~v

{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~f~{\textsf{vnil}}~v=\hbox{\pagecolor{RegalBlue!10}$\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textsf{map}}~f~v\equiv{\textsf{map}}~f~v$}

{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}~v=

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textsf{vcons}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{map}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{map}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{map}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{map}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv{\textsf{vcons}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{map}}~f~u\oplus{\textsf{map}}~f~v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{map}}~f~u\oplus{\textsf{map}}~f~v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{map}}~f~u\oplus{\textsf{map}}~f~v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{map}}~f~u\oplus{\textsf{map}}~f~v$}\mathclose{}{)}}

In our language, unfolding
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
has the side effect of
also
unfolding
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
: in
other words, unfolding is
transitive
. To see why this is the case, observe that the unfolding
of
{{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u}\oplus v:{\textsf{vec}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{{\textsf{su}}~m}+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{{\textsf{su}}~m}+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\textsf{su}}~m}+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{{\textsf{su}}~m}+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A
, namely

{\textsf{vcons}}~a~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}:{\textsf{vec}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{su}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{su}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{su}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{su}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A
, would otherwise not be
well-typed. From an implementation perspective, one can think of the transitivity of unfolding as
necessary for
subject reduction
. Having unfolded
map
,
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, and thus

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, we complete our definition:

{\textsf{cong}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to a\equiv a^{\prime}\to f~a\equiv f~a^{\prime}

{\textsf{cong}}~f~{\textsf{refl}}={\textsf{refl}}

{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~{\textbf{unfolds}}~{\textsf{map}};\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}

{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\to{\textsf{map}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv{\textsf{map}}~f~u\oplus{\textsf{map}}~f~v

{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~f~{\textsf{vnil}}~v={\textsf{refl}}

{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}~v={\textsf{cong}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{vcons}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~f~u~v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~f~u~v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~f~u~v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~f~u~v$}\mathclose{}{)}}

2.3
Recovering unconditionally transparent and opaque definitions

There are also times when we intend a given definition to be a fully transparent

abbreviation
, in the sense of being unfolded automatically whenever possible. We indicate
this with an
abbreviation
declaration:

abbreviation

singleton

{\textsf{singleton}}:A\to{\textsf{vec}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{su}}~{\textsf{ze}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{su}}~{\textsf{ze}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{su}}~{\textsf{ze}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{su}}~{\textsf{ze}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A

{\textsf{singleton}}~a={\textsf{vcons}}~a~{\textsf{vnil}}

Then the following lemma can be defined without any explicit unfolding:

{\textsf{abbrv-example}}:{\textsf{singleton}}~5\equiv{\textsf{vcons}}~5~{\textsf{vnil}}

{\textsf{abbrv-example}}={\textsf{refl}}

The meaning of the
abbreviation
keyword must account for unfolding constraints. For
instance, what would it mean to make
map-
\oplus
an abbreviation?

abbreviation

map-
\oplus

{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~{\textbf{unfolds}}~{\textsf{map}};\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}

…

We cannot unfold
map-
\oplus
in all contexts, because its definition is only well-typed
when
map
and
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
are unfolded. The meaning of this declaration must, therefore,
be that
map-
\oplus
shall be unfolded
just as soon as

map
and
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}

are unfolded. In other words,
{\textbf{abbreviation}}~\vartheta
followed by

\vartheta~{\textbf{unfolds}}~\kappa_{1};\ldots;\kappa_{n}
means that unfolding
\vartheta
is synonymous
with unfolding all of
\kappa_{1};\ldots;\kappa_{n}
.

Conversely, we may intend a given definition
never
to unfold, which we may indicate by a
corresponding
abstract
declaration. Because definitions in our system do not automatically unfold, the
force of
{\textbf{abstract}}~\vartheta
is simply to prohibit users from including
\vartheta
in any
subsequent
unfolds
annotations.

Remark 1

A slight variation on our system can recover the behavior of Agda’s
abstract
blocks by

limiting
the scope in which a definition
\vartheta
can be unfolded; the transitivity of
unfolding dictates that any definition
\vartheta^{\prime}
that unfolds
\vartheta
cannot itself be
unfolded once we leave that scope. We leave the details to future work.

2.4
Unfolding within the type

The effect of a
\vartheta~{\textbf{unfolds}}~\kappa_{1};\ldots;\kappa_{n}
declaration
is to make
\kappa_{1};\ldots\kappa_{n}
unfold within the
definition
of

\vartheta
, but still not within its type; it will happen, however, that a

type
might not be expressible without some unfolding. First we will show
how to accommodate this situation using only features we have introduced so
far, and then in
Section

2.5
we will
devise a more general and ergonomic solution.

Consider the left-unit law for
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
: in order to state that a vector
u
is equal to the
vector
{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus u
, we must contend with their differing types
{\textsf{vec}}~n~A
and

{\textsf{vec}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{ze}}+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{ze}}+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{ze}}+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{ze}}+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A
respectively. One approach is to rewrite along the left-unit law for

\mathbb{N}
; indeed, to state the
right-unit
law for
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
one must rewrite along
the right-unit law for
\mathbb{N}
. But here, because
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
computes on its first argument,

{\textsf{vec}}~n~A
and
{\textsf{vec}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{ze}}+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{ze}}+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{ze}}+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{ze}}+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A
would be definitionally equal types if we could
unfold
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

In order to formulate the left-unit law for
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, we start by defining its
type
as
an abbreviation that unfolds
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
:

abbreviation

\oplus
-left-unit-type

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit-type}}~{\textbf{unfolds}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit-type}}:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A\to{\textsf{Type}}

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit-type}}~u={\textsf{vnil}}\oplus u\equiv u

Now we may state the intended lemma using the type defined above:

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit-type}}~u

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}~u=\hbox{\pagecolor{RegalBlue!10}$\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit-type}}~u$}

Clearly we must unfold
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and thus
\oplus
-left-unit-type
to
simplify our goal:

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}~{\textbf{unfolds}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit-type}}~u

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}~u=\hbox{\pagecolor{RegalBlue!10}$\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus u\equiv u$}

We complete the proof by unfolding
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
itself, which transitively unfolds
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
:

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}~{\textbf{unfolds}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit-type}}~u

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}~u={\textsf{refl}}

2.5
Unfolding within subexpressions

We have just demonstrated how to unfold definitions within the
type
of a declaration by
defining that type as an additional declaration; using the same technique, we can introduce
unfoldings within
any subexpression
by hoisting that subexpression to a top-level definition
with its own unfolding constraint.

Unfolding within the type, revisited

Rather than repeating the somewhat verbose pattern of
Section

2.4
,
we abstract it as a new language feature that is easily eliminated by elaboration. In particular, we
introduce a new
expression
former
{\textbf{unfold}}~\kappa~{\textbf{in}}~M
that can be placed in any
expression context. Let us replay the example from
Section

2.4
, but
using
unfold
rather than an auxiliary definition:

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\textbf{unfold}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}~{\textbf{in}}}~{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus u\equiv u

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}~u=\hbox{\pagecolor{RegalBlue!10}$\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textbf{unfold}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}~{\textbf{in}}~{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus u\equiv u$}

The type
{\textbf{unfold}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}~{\textbf{in}}~{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus u\equiv u
is in
normal form; the only way to simplify it is to unfold
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. We could do
this with another inline
unfold
expression, but here we will use a
top-level declaration:

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}~{\textbf{unfolds}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}}

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\textbf{unfold}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}~{\textbf{in}}~{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus u\equiv u

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}~u=\hbox{\pagecolor{RegalBlue!10}$\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus u\equiv u$}

By virtue of the above, the
unfold
expression in our hole has computed
away and we are left with
\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus u\equiv u
as

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is still abstract in this scope. To make progress, we

strengthen
the declaration to unfold
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
in addition to

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
:

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}~{\textbf{unfolds}}~{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}}

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\textbf{unfold}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}~{\textbf{in}}~{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus u\equiv u

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}~u={\textsf{refl}}

The meaning of the code above is exactly as described in

Section

2.4
: the
unfold
scope
is elaborated to a new top-level
abbreviation
that unfolds

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

Expression-level
vs
.
top-level unfolding

We noted in our definition of
\oplus
-left-unit
above that we could have
replaced the top-level
{\textbf{unfolds}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
directive of
\oplus
-left-unit
with the
new expression-level
{\textbf{unfold}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}~{\textbf{in}}
as follows:

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}^{\prime}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\textbf{unfold}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}~{\textbf{in}}~{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus u\equiv u

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}^{\prime}~u={\textbf{unfold}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}~{\textbf{in}}~{\textsf{refl}}

The resulting definition of
{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}^{\prime}
has slightly different behavior than

\oplus
-left-unit
above: whereas unfolding
\oplus
-left-unit
causes
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}

to unfold transitively, we can unfold
{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}^{\prime}
without unfolding

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
—at the cost of
{\textbf{unfold}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
expressions appearing in our goal. This
more granular behavior may be desirable in some cases, and it is a strength of our language and its
elaborative semantics that the programmer can manipulate unfolding in such a fine-grained manner.

For completeness, we illustrate the elimination of expression-level unfolding from the definition of

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}^{\prime}
:

abbreviation

\oplus
-left-unit
′
-type

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit${}^{\prime}$-type}}~{\textbf{unfolds}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit${}^{\prime}$-type}}:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A\to{\textsf{Type}}

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit${}^{\prime}$-type}}~u={\textsf{vnil}}\oplus u\equiv u

abbreviation

\oplus
-left-unit
′
-body

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit${}^{\prime}$-body}}~{\textbf{unfolds}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit${}^{\prime}$-body}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit${}^{\prime}$-type}}~u

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit${}^{\prime}$-body}}~u={\textsf{refl}}

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}^{\prime}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit${}^{\prime}$-type}}~u

{\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit}}^{\prime}~u={\textsf{$\oplus$-left-unit${}^{\prime}$-body}}~u

In our experience, expression-level unfolding seems more commonly useful for end users than
top-level unfolding; on the other hand, the clearest semantics for expression-level unfolding are
stated in terms of top-level unfolding! Because one of our goals is to provide an account of
unfolding that admits a reliable and precise mental model for programmers, it is desirable to
include both top-level and expression-level unfolding in the surface language.

3
Controlling unfolding with extension types

Having introduced our new surface language constructs for controlled unfolding in

Section

2
, we now describe how to elaborate these constructs into our
dependently-typed core calculus. Again we proceed by example, deferring our formal descriptions of
the core calculus and elaboration algorithm to
Section

4
.

3.1
A dependently-typed core calculus with proposition symbols

Our core calculus parameterizes intensional Martin-Löf type theory (
MLTT
)

[
25
]
by a bounded meet semilattice of
proposition symbols

p\in\mathbb{P}
, and
adjoins to the type theory a new form of context extension and two new type formers
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,A

and
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle p\hookrightarrow M$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle p\hookrightarrow M$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p\hookrightarrow M$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p\hookrightarrow M$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
involving proposition symbols:

(contexts)

\Gamma

\Coloneqq

\dots{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\;\mid\;}{}\Gamma,p

(types)

A

\Coloneqq

\dots{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\;\mid\;}{}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,A{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\;\mid\;}{}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle p\hookrightarrow M$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle p\hookrightarrow M$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p\hookrightarrow M$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p\hookrightarrow M$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

The bounded meet semilattice structure on
\mathbb{P}
closes proposition symbols under conjunction
\land

and the true proposition
\top
, thereby partially ordering
\mathbb{P}
by entailment
p\leq q
(“
p

entails
q
”) satisfying the usual logical principles. We say
p
is
true
if
\top
entails

p
; the context extension
\Gamma,p
hypothesizes that
p
is true.

Remark 2

Our proposition symbols are much more restricted than, and should not be confused with, other
notions of proposition in type theory such as h-propositions
[
41
, §3.3]
or strict
propositions
[
16
]
. In particular, unlike types, our proposition symbols have no
associated proof terms.

The type
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,A
is the dependent product “
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\_:p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\_:p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\_:p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\_:p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\to A
”,
i.e
.
,
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,A
is
well-formed when
A
is a type under the hypothesis that
p
is true, and
f:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,A
when,
given that
p
is true, we may conclude
f:A
. The
extension type

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
is
well-formed when
A
is a type and
a_{p}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,A
; its elements
a:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
are terms

a:A
satisfying the side condition that when
p
is true, we have
a=a_{p}:A
.

3.2
Elaborating controlled unfolding to our core calculus

Our surface language extends a generic surface language for dependent type theory with a new
expression former
unfold
and several new declaration forms:

\vartheta~{\textbf{unfolds}}~\kappa_{1};\dots;\kappa_{n}
for controlled unfolding,

{\textbf{abbreviation}}~\vartheta
for transparent definitions, and
{\textbf{abstract}}~\vartheta
for
opaque definitions. Elaboration transforms these surface-language declarations into core-language

signatures
,
i.e
.
sequences of declarations over our core calculus of
MLTT
with
proposition symbols.

Our core-language signatures include the following declaration forms:

•

{\textbf{prop}}~p\leq q
introduces a fresh proposition symbol
p
such that
p
entails

q\in\mathbb{P}
;

•

{\textbf{prop}}~p=q
defines the proposition symbol
p
to be an abbreviation for
q\in\mathbb{P}
;

•

{\textbf{const}}~\vartheta:A
introduces a constant
\vartheta
of type
A
.

We now revisit our examples from
Section

2
, illustrating how they are
elaborated into our core calculus:

Plain definitions

Recall our unadorned definition of
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
from
Section

2
:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}

{\textsf{ze}}+n=n

{{\textsf{su}}~m}+n={\textsf{su}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}

We elaborate
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
into a sequence of declarations: first, we introduce a new proposition symbol

\bm{\Upsilon}_{+}
corresponding to the proposition that “
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
unfolds.” Next, we introduce a new
definition
\bm{\delta}_{+}:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
satisfying the defining clauses of
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
above, under
the (trivial) assumption of
\top
; finally, we introduce a new constant
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
involving the
extension type of
\bm{\delta}_{+}
along
\bm{\Upsilon}_{+}
.

{\textbf{prop}}~\bm{\Upsilon}_{+}\leq\top

\bm{\delta}_{+}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\top$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\top$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\top$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\top$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m\,n:\mathbb{N}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m\,n:\mathbb{N}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m\,n:\mathbb{N}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m\,n:\mathbb{N}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathbb{N}

\bm{\delta}_{+}~{\textsf{ze}}~n=n

\bm{\delta}_{+}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{su}}~m$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{su}}~m$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{su}}~m$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{su}}~m$}\mathclose{}{)}}~n={\textsf{su}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\bm{\delta}_{+}~m~n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\bm{\delta}_{+}~m~n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bm{\delta}_{+}~m~n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bm{\delta}_{+}~m~n$}\mathclose{}{)}}

{\textbf{const}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{+}\hookrightarrow\bm{\delta}_{+}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{+}\hookrightarrow\bm{\delta}_{+}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{+}\hookrightarrow\bm{\delta}_{+}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{+}\hookrightarrow\bm{\delta}_{+}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

Remark 3

In a serious implementation, it would be simple to induce
\bm{\delta}_{+}

to be pretty-printed as
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
in user-facing displays such as goals
and error messages.

Top-level unfolding

To understand why we have elaborated
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
in this way, let us examine how to elaborate
top-level unfolding declarations (
Section

2.1
):

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}~{\textbf{unfolds}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A\to{\textsf{vec}}~n~A\to{\textsf{vec}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A

{\textsf{vnil}}\oplus v=v

{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u\oplus v={\textsf{vcons}}~a~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}

To elaborate
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}~{\textbf{unfolds}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, we define the proposition symbol

\bm{\Upsilon}_{\oplus}
to entail
\bm{\Upsilon}_{+}
, capturing the idea that unfolding
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
always
causes
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
to unfold; in order to cause
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
to unfold in the body of
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, we
assume
\bm{\Upsilon}_{+}
in the definition of
\bm{\delta}_{\oplus}
. In full, we elaborate the definition of

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
as follows:

{\textbf{prop}}~\bm{\Upsilon}_{\oplus}\leq\bm{\Upsilon}_{+}

\bm{\delta}_{\oplus}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{+}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{+}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{+}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{+}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\textsf{vec}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A

\bm{\delta}_{\oplus}~{\textsf{vnil}}~v=v

\bm{\delta}_{\oplus}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}~v={\textsf{vcons}}~a~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\bm{\delta}_{\oplus}~u~v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\bm{\delta}_{\oplus}~u~v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bm{\delta}_{\oplus}~u~v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bm{\delta}_{\oplus}~u~v$}\mathclose{}{)}}

{\textbf{const}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{vec}}~m~A\to{\textsf{vec}}~n~A\to{\textsf{vec}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{\oplus}\hookrightarrow\bm{\delta}_{\oplus}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{vec}}~m~A\to{\textsf{vec}}~n~A\to{\textsf{vec}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{\oplus}\hookrightarrow\bm{\delta}_{\oplus}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{vec}}~m~A\to{\textsf{vec}}~n~A\to{\textsf{vec}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{\oplus}\hookrightarrow\bm{\delta}_{\oplus}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{vec}}~m~A\to{\textsf{vec}}~n~A\to{\textsf{vec}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m+n$}\mathclose{}{)}}~A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{\oplus}\hookrightarrow\bm{\delta}_{\oplus}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

Observe that the definition of
\bm{\delta}_{\oplus}
is well-typed because
\bm{\Upsilon}_{+}
is true in its
scope: thus the the extension type of

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
causes
{\textsf{ze}}+n
to be definitionally equal to
\bm{\delta}_{+}~{\textsf{ze}}~n
, which in turn is
defined to be
n
. The constraint
\bm{\Upsilon}_{\oplus}\hookrightarrow\bm{\delta}_{\oplus}
is well-typed
because
\bm{\Upsilon}_{\oplus}
entails
\bm{\Upsilon}_{+}
.

If a definition
\vartheta
unfolds multiple definitions
\kappa_{1};\dots;\kappa_{n}
, we define

\bm{\Upsilon}_{\vartheta}
to entail (and define
\bm{\delta}_{\vartheta}
to assume) the conjunction

\bm{\Upsilon}_{\kappa_{1}}\land\dots\land\bm{\Upsilon}_{\kappa_{n}}
; if a definition
\vartheta
unfolds no
definitions, then
\bm{\Upsilon}_{\vartheta}
entails (and
\bm{\delta}_{\vartheta}
assumes)
\top
, as in our

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}\mathclose{}{)}}
example.

Abbreviations

To elaborate the combination of the declarations
{\textbf{abbreviation}}~\vartheta
and

\vartheta~{\textbf{unfolds}}~\kappa_{1};\dots;\kappa_{n}
we define
\bm{\Upsilon}_{\vartheta}
to
equal
the
conjunction
\bm{\Upsilon}_{\kappa_{1}}\land\dots\land\bm{\Upsilon}_{\kappa_{n}}
. For example, consider
the following code from
Section

2.3
:

abbreviation

map-
\oplus

{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~{\textbf{unfolds}}~{\textsf{map}};\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}

{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}

{\textsf{map}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv{\textsf{map}}~f~u\oplus{\textsf{map}}~f~v

{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~f~{\textsf{vnil}}~v={\textsf{refl}}
