{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}~v={\textsf{cong}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{vcons}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~f~u~v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~f~u~v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~f~u~v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}~f~u~v$}\mathclose{}{)}}

It is elaborated as follows:

{\textbf{prop}}~\bm{\Upsilon}_{{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}}=\bm{\Upsilon}_{{\textsf{map}}}\land\bm{\Upsilon}_{\oplus}

\bm{\delta}_{{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{{\textsf{map}}}\land\bm{\Upsilon}_{\oplus}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{{\textsf{map}}}\land\bm{\Upsilon}_{\oplus}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{{\textsf{map}}}\land\bm{\Upsilon}_{\oplus}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{{\textsf{map}}}\land\bm{\Upsilon}_{\oplus}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\to{\textsf{map}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv{\textsf{map}}~f~u\oplus{\textsf{map}}~f~v

\bm{\delta}_{{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}}~f~{\textsf{vnil}}~v={\textsf{refl}}

\bm{\delta}_{{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~a~u$}\mathclose{}{)}}~v={\textsf{cong}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{vcons}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{vcons}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f~a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle const

\oplus

:\{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f:A\to B$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:{\textsf{vec}}~m~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v:{\textsf{vec}}~n~A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\textsf{map}}~f~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u\oplus v$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv{\textsf{map}}~f~u\oplus{\textsf{map}}~f~v\mid\bm{\Upsilon}_{{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}}\hookrightarrow\bm{\delta}_{{\textsf{map-$\oplus$}}}\}

Expression-level unfolding

The elaboration of the expression-level unfolding construct
{\textbf{unfold}}~\kappa~{\textbf{in}}~M
to our
core calculus factors through the elaboration of expression-level unfolding to top-level unfolding
as described in
Section

2.5
; we return to this in

Section

4.3
.

4
The elaboration algorithm

We now formally specify our mechanism for controlled unfolding by more precisely defining the
elaboration algorithm sketched in the previous section, starting with a precise definition of the
target of elaboration, our core calculus
TT
P
.

4.1
The core calculus
TT
P

Our core calculus
TT
P
is intensional Martin-Löf type theory (
MLTT
)
[
25
]

with dependent sums and products, natural numbers, a Tarski universe,
etc
.
, extended with (1) a
collection of proof-irrelevant proposition symbols, (2) dependent products over propositions, and
(3) extension types for those propositions
[
30
]
.

Remark 4

We treat the features of
MLTT
and of our surface language somewhat generically; our elaboration
algorithm can be applied on top of an existing bidirectional elaboration algorithm for type theory,

e.g
.
, those described in
[
11
,
21
]
.

In fact,
TT
P
is actually a family of type theories parameterized by a bounded meet semilattice

(\mathbb{P},\top,\land)
whose underlying set
\mathbb{P}
is the set of proposition symbols of
TT
P
; the
semilattice structure on
\mathbb{P}
axiomatizes the conjunctive fragment of propositional logic with

\land
as conjunction,
\top
as the true proposition, and
\leq
as entailment (where
p\leq q

is defined as
p\land q=p
), subject to the usual logical principles such as
p\land q\leq p
and

p\land q\leq q
and
p\leq\top
.

Remark 5

The judgments of
TT
P
are functorial in the choice of
\mathbb{P}
, in the sense that given any
homomorphism
f:\hbox to33.33pt{\vbox to7.85pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-0.33063pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

of bounded meet semilattices and any type or term in
TT
P

over
\mathbb{P}
, we have an induced type/term in
TT
{}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}
over
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
. In particular, we will use
the fact that judgments of
TT
P
are stable under adjoining new proposition symbols to
\mathbb{P}
.

The language
TT
P
augments ordinary
MLTT
with a new form of judgment
\Gamma\vdash p\ \mathit{true}
(for

p\in\mathbb{P}
) and the corresponding context extension
\Gamma,p
(for
p\in\mathbb{P}
). The
judgment
\Gamma\vdash p\ \mathit{true}
states that the proposition
p
is true in context
\Gamma
,
i.e
.
, the
conjunction of the propositional hypotheses in
\Gamma
entails
p
; the latter extends
\Gamma

with the hypothesis that
p
is true.

\frac{\Gamma\ \mathit{ctx}\quad p\in\mathbb{P}}{\Gamma,p\ \mathit{ctx}}\frac{p\in\mathbb{P}}{\Gamma,p\vdash p\ \mathit{true}}\frac{\Gamma,p\vdash{\mathcal{J}}\quad\Gamma\vdash p\ \mathit{true}}{\Gamma\vdash\mathcal{J}}\\
\frac{}{\Gamma\vdash\top\ \mathit{true}}\frac{\Gamma\vdash p\ \mathit{true}\quad\Gamma\vdash q\ \mathit{true}}{\Gamma\vdash p\land q\ \mathit{true}}\frac{\Gamma\vdash p\ \mathit{true}\quad p\leq q}{\Gamma\vdash q\ \mathit{true}}

The dependent product
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,A
of the family
p\vdash A
is defined as
an ordinary dependent product type, omitting the
\beta/\eta
rules for lack of space:

\frac{\Gamma,p\vdash A\ \mathit{type}}{\Gamma\vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,A\ \mathit{type}}\frac{\Gamma,p\vdash M:A}{\Gamma\vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}\,M:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,A}\frac{\Gamma\vdash M:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,A\quad\Gamma\vdash p\ \mathit{true}}{\Gamma\vdash M\mathbin{@}p:A}

The remaining feature of
TT
P
is the extension type
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
.
Given a proposition
p\in\mathbb{P}
and an element
a_{p}
of
A
under the hypothesis
p
,
the elements of
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
correspond to elements of
A
that equal
a_{p}

when
p
holds.

\frac{\Gamma\vdash A\ \mathit{type}\quad\Gamma,p\vdash a_{p}:A}{\Gamma\vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\ \mathit{type}}\frac{\Gamma\vdash a:A\quad\quad\Gamma,p\vdash a_{p}:A\quad\quad\Gamma,p\vdash a=a_{p}:A}{\Gamma\vdash{\textsf{in}}_{p}\,a:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}\frac{\Gamma\vdash a:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}{\Gamma\vdash{\textsf{out}}_{p}\,a:A}\frac{\Gamma\vdash a:A}{\Gamma\vdash{\textsf{out}}_{p}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{in}}_{p}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{in}}_{p}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{in}}_{p}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{in}}_{p}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}=a:A}\frac{\Gamma\vdash a:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}{\Gamma\vdash{\textsf{in}}_{p}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{out}}_{p}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{out}}_{p}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{out}}_{p}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{out}}_{p}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}=a:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle 4.2
Signatures over
TT
P

Our elaboration procedure takes as input a sequence of surface language definitions, and outputs a
well-formed
signature
, a list of declarations over
TT
P
.

(signatures)

\Sigma

\Coloneqq

\epsilon{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\;\mid\;}{}\Sigma,D

(declarations)

D

\Coloneqq

{\textbf{const}}\,x:A{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\;\mid\;}{}{\textbf{prop}}\,p\leq q{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\;\mid\;}{}{\textbf{prop}}\,p=q

A signature is well-formed precisely when each declaration in
\Sigma
is well-formed relative to
the earlier declarations in
\Sigma
. Our well-formedness judgment

\vdash\Sigma\,\mathit{sig}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P},\Gamma
computes from
\Sigma
the
TT
P
context
\Gamma
and
proposition semilattice
\mathbb{P}
specified by
\Sigma
’s
const
and
prop

declarations, respectively.

The rules for signature well-formedness are standard except for the
{\textbf{prop}}\,p\leq q
and

{\textbf{prop}}\,p=q
declarations, which extend
\mathbb{P}
with a new element
p
satisfying
p\leq q
or
p=q
respectively. Recalling that our core calculus
TT
P
is really a family of type
theories parameterized by a semilattice
\mathbb{P}
, these declarations shift us between type
theories,
e.g
.
, from
TT
P
to
TT
Q
, where
\mathbb{Q}=\mathbb{P}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle p\leq q$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle p\leq q$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p\leq q$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p\leq q$}\mathclose{}{]}}

is the minimal semilattice containing
\mathbb{P}
and an element
p
satisfying
p\leq q
. This
shifting between theories is justified by
Remark

5
.

\frac{}{\vdash\epsilon\,\mathit{sig}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\top$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\top$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\top$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\top$}\mathclose{}{\}}},\cdot}\frac{\vdash\Sigma\,\mathit{sig}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P},\Gamma\quad\Gamma\vdash_{\text{{{TT}${}_{\mathbb{P}}$}}}A\,\mathit{type}}{\vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Sigma,\ {\textbf{const}}\,x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Sigma,\ {\textbf{const}}\,x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Sigma,\ {\textbf{const}}\,x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Sigma,\ {\textbf{const}}\,x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,\mathit{sig}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma,x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Gamma,x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma,x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma,x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}\frac{\vdash\Sigma\,\mathit{sig}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P},\Gamma\quad q\in\mathbb{P}}{\vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Sigma,\ {\textbf{prop}}\,p\leq q$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Sigma,\ {\textbf{prop}}\,p\leq q$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Sigma,\ {\textbf{prop}}\,p\leq q$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Sigma,\ {\textbf{prop}}\,p\leq q$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,\mathit{sig}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle p\leq q$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle p\leq q$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p\leq q$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p\leq q$}\mathclose{}{]}},\Gamma\\
\vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Sigma,\ {\textbf{prop}}\,p=q$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Sigma,\ {\textbf{prop}}\,p=q$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Sigma,\ {\textbf{prop}}\,p=q$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Sigma,\ {\textbf{prop}}\,p=q$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,\mathit{sig}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle p=q$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle p=q$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p=q$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p=q$}\mathclose{}{]}},\Gamma}

4.3
Bidirectional elaboration

We adopt a bidirectional elaboration algorithm which mirrors bidirectional type-checking
algorithms
[
10
,
28
]
.
The top-level elaboration judgment
\Sigma\vdash\vec{S}\rightsquigarrow\Sigma^{\prime}
takes as input the current
well-formed signature
\Sigma
and a list of surface-level definitions
\vec{S}
and outputs a new
signature
\Sigma^{\prime}
, maintaining the invariant that successful elaboration produces well-formed
signatures.

We define
\Sigma\vdash\vec{S}\rightsquigarrow\Sigma^{\prime}
in terms of three auxiliary judgments for elaborating
surface-language types and terms; in the bidirectional style, we divide term elaboration into a
checking judgment
\Sigma;\Gamma\vdash\texttt{e}\Leftarrow A\rightsquigarrow\Sigma^{\prime},M
taking a core type as input, and a
synthesis judgment
\Sigma;\Gamma\vdash\texttt{e}\Rightarrow A\rightsquigarrow\Sigma^{\prime},M
producing a core type as output.
All three judgments take as input a signature
\Sigma
and a context (telescope) over
\Sigma
, and
output a new signature along with a core type or term.

In the algorithm, we write

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textbf{def}}~\vartheta:\texttt{A},\textit{abbrv?},\textit{abstr?},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\kappa_{1},\ldots\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}},\texttt{e}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textbf{def}}~\vartheta:\texttt{A},\textit{abbrv?},\textit{abstr?},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\kappa_{1},\ldots\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}},\texttt{e}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textbf{def}}~\vartheta:\texttt{A},\textit{abbrv?},\textit{abstr?},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\kappa_{1},\ldots\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}},\texttt{e}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textbf{def}}~\vartheta:\texttt{A},\textit{abbrv?},\textit{abstr?},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\kappa_{1},\ldots\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}},\texttt{e}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

for the representation of a surface-level definition
S
; in this expression,
\vartheta
is the
name of the definiendum,
A
is the surface-level type of the definition,
abbrv?
and

abstr?
are flags governing whether
\vartheta
is an
abbreviation
(resp., is

abstract
),
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
are the names of the definitions that

\vartheta
unfolds, and
e
is the surface-level definiens.

The elaboration judgment elaborates each surface definition in sequence:

\frac{\Sigma\vdash\vec{S}\rightsquigarrow\Sigma_{1}\quad\quad\Sigma_{1};\cdot\vdash\texttt{A}\Leftarrow\mathit{type}\rightsquigarrow\Sigma_{2},A\quad\quad\Sigma_{2};\mathchoice{\textstyle\bigwedge_{i\leq n}}{\textstyle\bigwedge_{i\leq n}}{\scriptstyle\bigwedge_{i\leq n}}{\scriptscriptstyle\bigwedge_{i\leq n}}{\bm{\Upsilon}_{\kappa_{i}}}\vdash\texttt{e}\Leftarrow A\rightsquigarrow\Sigma_{3},M\quad\quad{\textbf{let}}~{p}\coloneqq{\textbf{if}}~\textit{abstr?}~{\textbf{then}}~\textit{gensym}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}~{\textbf{else}}~\bm{\Upsilon}_{\vartheta}\quad\quad{\textbf{let}}~{\boxslash}\coloneqq{\textbf{if}}~\textit{abbrv?}~{\textbf{then}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle=$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle=$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle=$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle=$}\mathclose{}{)}}~{\textbf{else}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\leq$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\leq$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\leq$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\leq$}\mathclose{}{)}}\quad\quad{\textbf{let}}~\Sigma_{4}\coloneqq\Sigma_{3},{\textbf{prop}}\,p\mathrel{\boxslash}\mathchoice{\textstyle\bigwedge_{i\leq n}}{\textstyle\bigwedge_{i\leq n}}{\scriptstyle\bigwedge_{i\leq n}}{\scriptscriptstyle\bigwedge_{i\leq n}}{\bm{\Upsilon}_{\kappa_{i}}},{\textbf{const}}\,\vartheta:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle p\hookrightarrow M$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle p\hookrightarrow M$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p\hookrightarrow M$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p\hookrightarrow M$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}{\Sigma\vdash\vec{S},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textbf{def}}~\vartheta:\texttt{A},\textit{abbrv?},\textit{abstr?},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}},\texttt{e}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textbf{def}}~\vartheta:\texttt{A},\textit{abbrv?},\textit{abstr?},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}},\texttt{e}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textbf{def}}~\vartheta:\texttt{A},\textit{abbrv?},\textit{abstr?},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}},\texttt{e}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textbf{def}}~\vartheta:\texttt{A},\textit{abbrv?},\textit{abstr?},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}$}\mathclose{}{]}},\texttt{e}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\rightsquigarrow\Sigma_{4}}

Remark 6

When a definition is marked
abstract
, the name of the
unfolding proposition is generated fresh so that it cannot be accessed by any future

unfold
declaration. Conversely, when a definition is marked as an

abbreviation
, its unfolding proposition is defined to be equivalent to the
conjunction of its dependencies rather than merely entailing its dependencies.

The rules for term and type elaboration are largely standard,
e.g
.
, we elaborate a surface dependent
product to a core dependent product by recursively elaborating the first and second components. We
single out two cases below: the boundary between checking and synthesis, and the expression-level

unfold
.

\frac{\Sigma;\Gamma\vdash\texttt{e}\Rightarrow A\rightsquigarrow\Sigma_{1};M\quad\quad\Sigma_{1};\Gamma\vdash{\textsf{conv}}\,A\,B}{\Sigma;\Gamma\vdash\texttt{e}\Leftarrow B\rightsquigarrow\Sigma_{1};M}\frac{\Sigma;\Gamma,\bm{\Upsilon}_{\vartheta}\vdash\texttt{e}\Leftarrow A\rightsquigarrow\Sigma_{1};M\quad{\textbf{let}}~\chi\coloneqq\textit{gensym}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}\quad\quad{\textbf{let}}~\Sigma_{2}\coloneqq\Sigma_{1},{\textbf{const}}\,\chi:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{\Gamma}}{\textstyle\prod_{\Gamma}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{\Gamma}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{\Gamma}}{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{\vartheta}\hookrightarrow M$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{\vartheta}\hookrightarrow M$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{\vartheta}\hookrightarrow M$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\bm{\Upsilon}_{\vartheta}\hookrightarrow M$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}}{\Sigma;\Gamma\vdash{\textbf{unfold}}~\vartheta~{\textbf{in}}~\texttt{e}\Leftarrow A\rightsquigarrow\Sigma_{2};{\textsf{out}}_{\bm{\Upsilon}_{\vartheta}}\,\chi\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{]}}}

The first rule states that a term synthesizing a type
A
can be checked against a type
B
provided
that
A
and
B
are definitionally equal; in order to implement this rule algorithmically, we need
definitional equality to be decidable. Additionally, our (omitted) type-directed elaboration rules
are only well-defined if type constructors are injective up to definitional equality,
e.g
.
,
A\to B=C\to D
if and only if
A=C
and
B=D
. We establish both of these essential properties of
definitional equality in
Section

6
.

Elaborating expression-level unfolding requires the ability to hoist a type to the top level by
iterating dependent products over its context, an operation notated
\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{\Gamma}}{\textstyle\prod_{\Gamma}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{\Gamma}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{\Gamma}}
above.
Because
\Gamma
can hypothesize (the truth of) propositions, this operation relies crucially on the
presence of dependent products
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,A
.

5
Case study: an implementation in
cool
tt

We have implemented our approach to controlled unfolding in the experimental
cool
tt
proof
assistant
[
29
]
;
cool
tt
is an implementation of
cartesian cubical type
theory

[
2
]
, a computational version of homotopy type theory whose syntactic
metatheory is particularly well understood
[
31
,
33
]
. The existing support
for partial elements and extension types made
cool
tt
a particularly hospitable ground to
experiment with elaborating controlled unfolding to extension types. The following example
illustrates the use of controlled unfolding in
cool
tt
:

{\textbf{def}}~{+}:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}\coloneqq

elim

\mid{\textsf{zero}}\Rightarrow{n\Rightarrow n}

\mid{\textsf{suc}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\_\Rightarrow\mathit{ih}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\_\Rightarrow\mathit{ih}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\_\Rightarrow\mathit{ih}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\_\Rightarrow\mathit{ih}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\Rightarrow{n\Rightarrow{\textsf{suc}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathit{ih}\,n$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathit{ih}\,n$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathit{ih}\,n$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathit{ih}\,n$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}

{\textbf{unfold}}~{+}

{\textbf{def}}~{\textsf{+0L}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x:\mathbb{N}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x:\mathbb{N}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x:\mathbb{N}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x:\mathbb{N}$}\mathclose{}{)}}:{\textsf{path}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{+}~0~x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle{+}~0~x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{+}~0~x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{+}~0~x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}~x\coloneqq

i\Rightarrow x

{{\textbf{def}}~{\textsf{+0R}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x:\mathbb{N}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x:\mathbb{N}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x:\mathbb{N}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x:\mathbb{N}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\textsf{path}}~\mathbb{N}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{+}~x~0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle{+}~x~0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{+}~x~0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{+}~x~0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}~x\coloneqq}

elim

\mid{\textsf{zero}}\Rightarrow{\textsf{+0L}}~0

\mid{\textsf{suc}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\Rightarrow\mathit{ih}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle x\Rightarrow\mathit{ih}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\Rightarrow\mathit{ih}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\Rightarrow\mathit{ih}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\Rightarrow

{\textbf{equation}}~\mathbb{N}

\begin{array}[t]{ll}\mid{+}~0~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}&{=}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{+0L}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{+0L}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{+0L}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{+0L}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{suc}}~y$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}\\
\mid{\textsf{suc}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{+}~x~0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle{+}~x~0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{+}~x~0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{+}~x~0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}&{=}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle i\Rightarrow{\textsf{suc}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathit{ih}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathit{ih}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathit{ih}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathit{ih}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle i\Rightarrow{\textsf{suc}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathit{ih}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathit{ih}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathit{ih}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathit{ih}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i\Rightarrow{\textsf{suc}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathit{ih}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathit{ih}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathit{ih}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathit{ih}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i\Rightarrow{\textsf{suc}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathit{ih}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathit{ih}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathit{ih}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathit{ih}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}\\
\mid{\textsf{suc}}~x\end{array}

This example follows a common pattern: we prove basic computational laws (
+0L
) by unfolding
a definition, and then in subsequent results (
+0R
) use these lemmas abstractly rather than
unfolding. Doing so controls the size and readability of proof goals, and explicitly demarcates
which parts of the library depend on the definitional behavior of a given function.

We have also implemented the derived forms for expression-level unfolding:

{\textbf{def}}~{\textsf{two}}:\mathbb{N}\coloneqq{+}~1~1

{\textbf{def}}~{\textsf{thm}}:{\textsf{path}}~\mathbb{N}~{\textsf{two}}~2\coloneqq{\textbf{unfold}}~{\textsf{two}}~{+}~{\textbf{in}}~i\Rightarrow 2

{\textbf{def}}~{\textsf{thm-is-refl}}:{\textsf{path-p}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle i\Rightarrow{\textsf{path}}~\mathbb{N}~{\textsf{two}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{thm}}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{thm}}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{thm}}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{thm}}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle i\Rightarrow{\textsf{path}}~\mathbb{N}~{\textsf{two}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{thm}}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{thm}}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{thm}}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{thm}}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i\Rightarrow{\textsf{path}}~\mathbb{N}~{\textsf{two}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{thm}}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{thm}}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{thm}}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{thm}}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i\Rightarrow{\textsf{path}}~\mathbb{N}~{\textsf{two}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{thm}}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{thm}}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{thm}}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{thm}}~i$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle i\Rightarrow{\textsf{two}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle i\Rightarrow{\textsf{two}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i\Rightarrow{\textsf{two}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i\Rightarrow{\textsf{two}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}~{\textsf{thm}}\coloneqq

i~j\Rightarrow{\textbf{unfold}}~{\textsf{two}}~{+}~{\textbf{in}}~2

{\textbf{def}}~{\textsf{thm-is-refl'}}:{\textsf{path}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{path}}~\mathbb{N}~{\textsf{two}}~2$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{path}}~\mathbb{N}~{\textsf{two}}~2$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{path}}~\mathbb{N}~{\textsf{two}}~2$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{path}}~\mathbb{N}~{\textsf{two}}~2$}\mathclose{}{\}}}~\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle i\Rightarrow{\textbf{unfold}}~{\textsf{two}}~{+}~{\textbf{in}}~{\textsf{two}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle i\Rightarrow{\textbf{unfold}}~{\textsf{two}}~{+}~{\textbf{in}}~{\textsf{two}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i\Rightarrow{\textbf{unfold}}~{\textsf{two}}~{+}~{\textbf{in}}~{\textsf{two}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i\Rightarrow{\textbf{unfold}}~{\textsf{two}}~{+}~{\textbf{in}}~{\textsf{two}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}~{\textsf{thm}}\coloneqq

i~j\Rightarrow{\textbf{unfold}}~{\textsf{two}}~{+}~{\textbf{in}}~2

The third and fourth declarations above illustrate two strategies in
cool
tt
for dealing with a
dependent type whose well-formedness depends on an unfolding; in
thm-is-refl
we use a
dependent path type but only unfold in the definiens, whereas in
thm-is-refl’
we use a
non-dependent path type but must unfold in both the definiens and in its type.

Our
cool
tt
implementation deviates in a few respects from the presentation in this
paper: in particular, the propositions
\bm{\Upsilon}_{\kappa}
are
represented by abstract elements
i_{\kappa}:\mathbb{I}
of the interval via
the embedding
\mathbb{I}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{F}
sending
i
to
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i=_{\mathbb{I}}1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i=_{\mathbb{I}}1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i=_{\mathbb{I}}1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i=_{\mathbb{I}}1$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. This
implementation-specific design decision is not essential, but it enabled us to
use an existing solver for inequalities
i\leq_{\mathbb{I}}j
.
Our modifications to
cool
tt
were relatively modest, resulting in a net
increase of 996 lines of OCaml code; we believe that our implementation strategy is
also applicable in principle to systems such as Agda, which already features
the extension types that we need.

6
The metatheory of
TT
P

In
Section

4
we described an algorithm elaborating a surface language with controlled
unfolding to
TT
P
. In order to actually execute our algorithm, it is necessary to decide the
definitional equality of types in
TT
P
; as is often the case in type theory, type dependency
ensures that deciding equality for types also requires us to decide the equality of terms. In order
to implement our elaboration algorithm, we therefore prove a
normalization
result for

TT
P
.

At its heart, a normalization algorithm is a computable bijection between equivalence classes of
terms up to definitional equality and a collection of normal forms. By ensuring that the equality of
normal forms is evidently decidable, this yields an effective decision procedure for definitional
equality. In our case, we attack normalization through a
synthetic
and
semantic

approach to normalization by evaluation called synthetic Tait computability

[
34
,
33
,
31
,
32
]
or STC.

Neutral forms for
TT
P

This appealingly simple story for normalization is substantially complicated by the
\eta
-law
for extension types:

\frac{\Gamma\vdash p\ \mathit{true}\quad\Gamma,p\vdash a_{p}:A\quad\Gamma\vdash a:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p\hookrightarrow a_{p}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}{\Gamma\vdash{\textsf{out}}_{p}\,a=a_{p}:A}

When defining normal forms for
TT
P
, we might naively add a neutral form
{\color[rgb]{0.5273,0.0039,0.0664}\textbf{{out}}}_{p}
to represent

{\textsf{out}}_{p}
. In order to ensure that normal and neutral forms correspond bijectively with equivalence
classes of terms, however, we should only allow
{\color[rgb]{0.5273,0.0039,0.0664}\textbf{{out}}}_{p}
to be applied in a context where
p
is
not true; if
p
were true, then
{\textsf{out}}_{p}\,{a}
is already represented by the normal form for
a_{p}
.

A similar problem arises in the context of cubical type theory
[
9
,
2
]
where
some equalities apply precisely when two
dimensions
coincide. The same problem then presents
itself: either renamings must exclude substitutions which identify two dimension terms, or neutral
forms will fail to be stable under renamings.

In their recent proof of normalization for cubical type theory, Sterling and
Angiuli
[
33
]
refined neutral forms to account for this tension by introducing

stabilized neutrals
. Rather than cutting down on renamings, they expand the class of
neutrals by allowing “bad” neutrals akin to
{\color[rgb]{0.5273,0.0039,0.0664}\textbf{{out}}}_{p}\,e
in a context where
p
is
true. They then associate each neutral form with a
frontier of instability
: a proposition
which is true if the neutral is no longer meaningful. Crucially, while well-behaved neutrals may not
be stable under renamings, the frontier of instability
is
stable, and can therefore be incorporated into
the internal language.

We show that Sterling and Angiuli’s stabilized neutrals can be adapted to

TT
P
and use their approach to establish normalization. In so doing, we
refine Sterling and Angiuli’s approach to obtain a constructive normalization
proof. We also carefully spell out the details of the universe in the
normalization model, correcting an oversight in an earlier revision of Sterling’s
dissertation
[
31
]
.

6.1
Type theories as categories with representable maps

While any number of logical frameworks are available (generalized algebraic
theories
[
7
]
, essentially algebraic theories
[
15
]
, locally cartesian
closed categories
[
20
]
,
etc
.
), Uemura’s
categories with
representable maps

[
39
,
40
]
are particularly attractive because they express
exactly the binding and dependency structure needed for type theory: a second-order version of
generalized algebraic theories.

Definition 1

A
category with representable maps

\mathcal{C}
is a finitely complete category equipped with a
pullback-stable class of
representable maps

\mathcal{R}\subseteq\mathsf{Arr}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathcal{C}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathcal{C}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{C}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{C}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
such that
pullback along
f\in\mathcal{R}
has a right adjoint (dependent product along
f
).

We will often refer to categories with representable maps as
CwRs
.

Definition 2

A
morphism of CwRs
is a functor between the underlying categories that
preserves finite limits, representability of maps, and dependent products
along representable maps.

Definition 3

Categories with representable maps, morphisms between them, and natural isomorphisms assemble into
a
(2,1)
-category
\mathbf{CwR}
.

Uemura’s logical framework axiomatizes the category of judgments of
TT
P

as a particular category with representable maps
\mathbb{T}
. The finite limit
structure of
\mathbb{T}
encodes substitution as well as equality judgments, while
the class of representable maps carves out those judgments that may be
hypothesized. Uemura
[
39
]
develops a syntactic method for
presenting a CwR as a signature within a variant of extensional type theory,
which he has rephrased in terms of
second order generalized algebraic
theories
in his doctoral dissertation
[
40
]
. Although we will use
the type-theoretic presentation for convenience, the difference between these
two accounts is only superficial.

Each judgment of
TT
P
is rendered as a (dependent) sort while type and
term formers are modeled by elements of the given sorts. In order to record
whether a given judgment may be hypothesized, the sorts of the type theory
are stratified by
meta
-sorts
\star\subseteq\Box
where
A:\star

signifies that
A
is a representable sort (
i.e
.
a context-former) and can be
hypothesized, whereas
B:\Box
cannot parameterize a framework-level
dependent product.

Theorem 6.1

Let
\mathbb{T}
be the free category with representable maps generated by a given logical
framework signature;
then the groupoid of CwR functors
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{T},\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{T},\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{T},\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{T},\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{\mathbf{CwR}}
is equivalent to
the groupoid of interpretations of the signature within
\mathcal{E}
.

We will often refer to a category with representable maps
\mathbb{T}
as a
type theory; indeed, as the category of judgments of a given type theory,

\mathbb{T}
is a suitable invariant replacement for it.

The (2,1)-category of models of a type theory

Theorem

6.1
describes the
universal property of a type theory generated by a given signature in a logical
framework. Type theories
qua
CwRs thus give rise to a form of
functorial semantics in which algebras (interpretations) arrange into a

groupoid
of CwR functors
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{T},\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{T},\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{T},\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{T},\mathcal{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}_{\mathbf{CwR}}
.

This is an appropriate setting for studying the syntax of type theory, but it
is somewhat inappropriate for studying the
semantics
of type theory—in
which one expects models to correspond to structured
CwFs
[
13
]
or natural models
[
5
]
, which
themselves arrange into a (2,1)-category. The second notion of
functorial semantics, developed by Uemura in his doctoral
dissertation
[
40
]
, is a generalization of the theory of CwFs and
pseudo-morphisms between them
[
8
,
27
]
.

Note that we may always regard a presheaf category
\mathbf{Pr}(\mathcal{C})
as a CwR with the representable maps
being representable natural transformations,
i.e
.
families of presheaves whose fibers at
representables are representable
[
5
]
.

A
model
of a type theory
\mathbb{T}
is defined to be a
category
\mathcal{M}_{\diamond}
together with a CwR functor

For each CwR
\mathbb{T}
, Uemura has shown the following theorem:

Theorem 6.2

The (2,1)-category of models
{\textbf{Mod}}\,\mathbb{T}
has a bi-initial object
\mathcal{I}
.

Remark 7

If one takes
\mathbb{T}
to be
e.g
.
, Martin-Löf type theory, the bi-initial model
\mathcal{I}
can be
realized by the familiar initial CwF built from the category of contexts.

6.2
Encoding
TT
P
in the logical framework

We begin by defining the signature for a category with representable maps

\mathbb{T}_{0}
containing exactly the bare judgmental structure of
TT
P
, namely the
propositions and the judgments for types and terms. In our signature, we make
liberal use of the Agda-style notation for implicit arguments. As always,
p

ranges over
\mathbb{P}
.

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}:\star

\_:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle u,v:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle u,v:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u,v:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u,v:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\Longrightarrow u=v

{\textsf{tp}}:\square

{{\textsf{tm}}}:{\textsf{tp}}\Longrightarrow\star

We next extend the above to include the type formers of
TT
P
,
writing
\mathbb{T}
for the CwR generated by the full signature.

{notation}

Given
X:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\_:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\_:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\_:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\_:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\Longrightarrow\square
, we will write
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,X

to further abbreviate the Agda-style implicit function space
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\_:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\_:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\_:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\_:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\to X
.

These clauses precisely axiomatize the rules of extension types given in

Section

4.1
. Once the signature is complete, we obtain from Uemura’s
framework a category with representable maps
\mathbb{T}
together with a bi-initial model
\mathcal{I}
.

6.3
The atomic figure shape and its universal property

For each context
\Gamma
and type
\Gamma\vdash A\ \mathit{type}
, it is possible to
axiomatize the normal forms of type
A
; unfortunately, this assignment of sets
of normal forms does not immediately extend to a presheaf on the category of
contexts
\mathcal{I}_{\diamond}
, precisely because normal forms are not
a
priori
closed under substitution! In fact, closing normal forms under
substitution is the purpose of the normalization theorem, so we are not
able to assume it beforehand.

Normal forms
are
, however, closed under substitutions of variables for
variables (often called
structural renamings
), and in our case we shall
be able to close them additionally under the “phase transitions”

\Gamma,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
\Gamma,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle q$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle q$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle q$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle q$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
when
\Gamma,p\vdash q\ \mathit{true}
is derivable.
We shall refer to the substitutions so-restricted as
atomic
substitutions
, and we wish to organize them into a category.

It is possible to inductively define a category of “atomic contexts” whose objects are those of

\mathcal{I}_{\diamond}
and whose morphisms are atomic substitutions, but this construction obscures a
beautiful and simple (2,1)-categorical universal property first exposed by Bocquet, Kaposi, and
Sattler
[
6
]
that leads to a more modular proof. To explicate this
universal property, first note that the theory
\mathbb{T}_{0}
axiomatizes exactly the structure of
variables and phase transitions, and that the initial model
\mathcal{I}
of
\mathbb{T}
is, by restriction
along
\mathbb{T}_{0}
\mathbb{T}
, also a model of
\mathbb{T}_{0}
.

Definition 5

An
atomic substitution algebra
is given by a model
\mathcal{A}
of the
bare judgmental theory
\mathbb{T}_{0}
, together with a morphism of models

We use the bi-initial atomic substitution algebra as a
figure
shape
in the sense of Sterling
[
31
, §4.3]
to instantiate
synthetic Tait computability. Here we transition into the 2-category of
Grothendieck topoi, geometric morphisms, and geometric transformations, guided
by a
phase distinction
between “object-space” and
“meta-space”
[
32
]
; object-space refers to the object
language embodied in the model
\mathcal{I}
, whereas meta-space refers to the
metalanguage embodied in the model
\mathcal{A}
. Later on, we will construct a

glued
topos in which we may speak of constructs that have extent in both
object-space and meta-space. We follow Anel and Joyal
[
1
]
and
Vickers
[
42
]
in emphasizing the distinction between a topos

X
and the category of sheaves
\bm{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textsf{{X}}}}
presenting it:

Definition 6

We denote by
I
and
A
the
object-space
and
meta-space topoi

respectively, with underlying categories of sheaves
\bm{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textsf{{I}}}}=\mathbf{Pr}(\mathcal{I}_{\diamond})
and

\bm{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textsf{{A}}}}=\mathbf{Pr}(\mathcal{A}_{\diamond})
.

6.4
The language of synthetic Tait computability

As
I
and
A
are both subtopoi of
G
, they are reflected
in the internal language of
\bm{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textsf{{G}}}}
by means of a pair of complementary
lex idempotent monads (
🌕
,
🌑
). The internal language of

\bm{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textsf{{I}}}}
is presented by the
🌕
-modal or
object-space
types
and
\bm{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textsf{{A}}}}
is presented by
🌑
-modal or
meta-space
types.
Because they form an open/closed partition, these modal subuniverses admit a
particularly simple formulation:

Theorem 6.4

There exists a proposition
\mathsf{obj}:\Omega
such that

1.

a type
X
is
🌕
-modal / object-space iff
X\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{obj}\to X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{obj}\to X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\to X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\to X$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is an isomorphism;

2.

a type
X
is
🌑
-modal / meta-space iff
\mathsf{obj}\times X\to\mathsf{obj}
is an isomorphism.

Remark 8

We will use extension types
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
in the internal language of
\bm{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textsf{{G}}}}
as
realized by the subset comprehension of topos logic, treating their introduction and elimination
rules silently. Here the proposition
\phi
will be an element of the subobject classifier, in
contrast to the situation in our object language, where it ranged over a fixed set of proposition
symbols.

Remark 9

We isolate a subuniverse
\Omega_{\textit{dec}}\subseteq\Omega
of the subobject classifier that is closed under
finite disjunctions and contains
\mathsf{obj}
;
\Omega_{\textit{dec}}
will ultimately be a subuniverse spanned by
pointwise/externally decidable propositions
[
2
]
, but this fact will not play a
role in the synthetic development.

{notation}

As there is at most one element of
\mathsf{obj}
, we will reuse
Section

6.2

and write
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{obj}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{obj}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{obj}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{obj}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,A
rather than
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\_:\mathsf{obj}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\_:\mathsf{obj}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\_:\mathsf{obj}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\_:\mathsf{obj}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\to A
when
A:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\_:\mathsf{obj}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\_:\mathsf{obj}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\_:\mathsf{obj}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\_:\mathsf{obj}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\to\mathcal{U}
.

As a presheaf topos,
\bm{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textsf{{G}}}}
inherits a hierarchy of cumulative universes

\mathcal{U}_{i}
, each of which supports the
strict gluing
or

(mixed-phase) refinement

type
[
19
,
32
]
: a version of the
dependent sum of a family of meta-space types indexed in an object-space type

A
that
additionally
restricts within
object-space to exactly
A
:

\frac{A:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{obj}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{obj}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{obj}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{obj}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,\mathcal{U}_{i}\quad a:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle B\,a\cong\mathbf{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle Remark 10

In topos logic, it is a
property
for a function to have an inverse; thus we have
conveniently packaged the introduction and elimination rules for
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ltimes B\,x
into a single
function
gl
that is assumed to be an isomorphism.

{notation}

We write
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow a$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle b$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow a$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle b$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow a$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle b$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow a$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle b$}\mathclose{}{]}}
for
{\textsf{gl}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a,b$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and
\mathsf{ungl}\,x
for

\pi_{2}\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{gl}}^{-1}x$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big)}{\big)}{\big)}{)}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{gl}}^{-1}x$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big)}{\big)}{\big)}{)}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{gl}}^{-1}x$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big)}{\big)}{\big)}{)}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big(}{\big(}{\big(}{(}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{gl}}^{-1}x$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big)}{\big)}{\big)}{)}}}
.

Both
🌕
and
🌑
induce reflective subuniverses

\mathcal{U}_{\textnormal{\fullmoon}}^{i},\mathcal{U}_{\textnormal{\newmoon}}^{i}
\mathcal{U}_{i}
spanned by modal types, and these universes are
themselves modal. Following Sterling
[
31
]
, we use strict gluing to choose these
universes with additional strict properties:

\mathcal{U}_{\textnormal{\fullmoon}}^{i}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{i+1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{U}_{i}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathcal{U}_{i+1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{U}_{i}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{U}_{i+1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{U}_{i}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{U}_{i+1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{U}_{i}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\qquad\mathcal{U}_{\textnormal{\newmoon}}^{i}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{i+1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathcal{U}_{i+1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{U}_{i+1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{U}_{i+1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{1}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

Furthermore, the inclusion
\mathcal{U}_{\textnormal{\fullmoon}}^{i}
\mathcal{U}_{i}
restricts to the identity under

\mathsf{obj}
. With the modal universes to hand, we may choose
\textnormal{\fullmoon}:\mathcal{U}_{i}\to\mathcal{U}_{i}
and

\textnormal{\newmoon}:\mathcal{U}_{i}\to\mathcal{U}_{i}
to factor through
\mathcal{U}_{\textnormal{\fullmoon}}^{i}
and
\mathcal{U}_{\textnormal{\newmoon}}^{i}

respectively. Henceforth we will suppress the inclusions

\mathcal{U}_{\textnormal{\fullmoon}}^{i},\mathcal{U}_{\textnormal{\newmoon}}^{i}
\mathcal{U}_{i}
and write
e.g
.

\textnormal{\fullmoon}:\mathcal{U}_{i}\to\mathcal{U}_{\textnormal{\fullmoon}}^{i}
for the reflections.

Remark 11

The strict gluing types, modal universes, and their modal reflections can be
chosen to commute strictly with the liftings
\mathcal{U}_{i}
\mathcal{U}_{i+1}
.

TT
P
signature within
\bm{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textsf{{I}}}}

\bm{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textsf{{G}}}}

\mathcal{U}_{\textnormal{\fullmoon}}^{0}

Following
Remark

10
, we package the pair
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{in}}_{p},{\textsf{out}}_{p}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{in}}_{p},{\textsf{out}}_{p}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{in}}_{p},{\textsf{out}}_{p}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{in}}_{p},{\textsf{out}}_{p}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
as a
single isomorphism
{\textsf{in}}_{p}
.

The presheaf of terms in the model
\mathcal{A}
internalizes as a meta-space type
of
variables
which by virtue of the structure map
\mathcal{A}
\mathcal{I}
can be indexed over
the object-space collection of terms. We realize this synthetically as follows:

{\textsf{var}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathcal{U}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathcal{U}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{U}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{U}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

We refer to extensional type theory extended with these constants and modalities as the
language of
synthetic Tait computability
(STC).

Remark 12

To account for strict universes—those for which
el
commutes strictly with chosen
codes—some prior STC developments employed strict gluing along the image of

el

[
33
,
31
]
. By limiting our usage of strict gluing to
\mathsf{obj}
, we
are able to execute our constructions in a constructive metatheory. To model strict universes, we
instead rely on the cumulativity of the hierarchy of universes
\mathcal{U}_{i}
and the fact that all
levels are coherently closed under modalities and strict gluing.

6.5
Normal and neutral forms

Internally to STC, we now specify the normal and neutral forms of terms, and
the normal forms of types. Following Sterling and Angiuli
[
33
]

we index the type of neutral forms by a
frontier of instability
, a
proposition at which the neutral form is no longer meaningful. Our
construction proceeds in two steps. First, we define a series of indexed
quotient-inductive definitions
[
24
]
specifying the
meta-space components of normal and neutral forms:

{{\textsf{nf}}}_{\bullet}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A\to\mathcal{U}_{\textnormal{\newmoon}}^{0}

{{\textsf{ne}}}_{\bullet}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\Omega_{\textit{dec}}\to{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A\to\mathcal{U}_{\textnormal{\newmoon}}^{0}

{\textsf{nftp}}_{\bullet}:{\textsf{tp}}\to\mathcal{U}_{\textnormal{\newmoon}}^{0}

Next we use the strict gluing connective to define the types of normals, neutrals, and normal types
such that they lie strictly over
tm
and
tp
:

{{\textsf{nf}}}\,A=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ltimes{{\textsf{nf}}}_{\bullet}\,A\,a

{{\textsf{ne}}}_{\phi}\,A=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ltimes{{\textsf{ne}}}_{\bullet}\,A\,\phi\,a

{\textsf{nftp}}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ltimes{\textsf{nftp}}_{\bullet}\,A

We illustarte a representative fragment of the inductive definitions in
Fig.

1
.

The induction principles for
{{\textsf{nf}}}_{\bullet},{{\textsf{ne}}}_{\bullet}
and
{\textsf{nftp}}_{\bullet}
play
no role in the main development, which works with
any
algebra for these
constants. These induction principles, however, are needed in order to to prove

Theorem

6.6
and deduce the decidability of
definitional equality and the injectivity of type constructors. These same
considerations motivate our choice to index
{{\textsf{ne}}}_{\bullet}
over
\Omega_{\textit{dec}}

rather than
\Omega
.
