6.6
The normalization model

The construction of the normalization model itself is now reduced to a series of programming
exercises. Specifically, we must give a new
TT
P
-algebra internally to
\bm{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textsf{{G}}}}
subject to
the constraint that each of its constituents restricts under
\mathsf{obj}
to the corresponding constant
from the
TT
P
-algebra inherited from
\bm{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textsf{{I}}}}
. For instance, we must define types
representing object types and terms:

{\textsf{tp}}^{*}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{2}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathcal{U}_{2}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{U}_{2}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{U}_{2}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\qquad{{\textsf{tm}}^{*}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{tp}}^{*}\to\mathcal{U}_{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{{\textsf{tm}}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{tp}}^{*}\to\mathcal{U}_{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{{\textsf{tm}}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{tp}}^{*}\to\mathcal{U}_{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{{\textsf{tm}}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{tp}}^{*}\to\mathcal{U}_{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{{\textsf{tm}}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

The meta-space component of the computability structure of types is given as a
dependent record below:

{\textbf{record}}\ {\textsf{tp}}_{\bullet}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathcal{U}_{2}\ {\textbf{where}}

{\textsf{code}}:{\textsf{nftp}}_{\bullet}\,A

{\textsf{tm}}_{\bullet}:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A\to\mathcal{U}_{\textnormal{\newmoon}}^{1}

{\textsf{reflect}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi:\Omega_{\textit{dec}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi:\Omega_{\textit{dec}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi:\Omega_{\textit{dec}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi:\Omega_{\textit{dec}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle e:{{\textsf{ne}}}_{\bullet}\,A\,\phi\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle e:{{\textsf{ne}}}_{\bullet}\,A\,\phi\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle e:{{\textsf{ne}}}_{\bullet}\,A\,\phi\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle e:{{\textsf{ne}}}_{\bullet}\,A\,\phi\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a_{\phi}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,{\textsf{tm}}_{\bullet}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a_{\phi}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,{\textsf{tm}}_{\bullet}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a_{\phi}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,{\textsf{tm}}_{\bullet}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a_{\phi}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,{\textsf{tm}}_{\bullet}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{tm}}_{\bullet}\,a$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi\hookrightarrow a_{\phi}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{tm}}_{\bullet}\,a$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a_{\phi}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{tm}}_{\bullet}\,a$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a_{\phi}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{tm}}_{\bullet}\,a$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a_{\phi}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

{\textsf{reify}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to{\textsf{tm}}_{\bullet}\,a\to{{\textsf{nf}}}_{\bullet}\,A\,a

The
{\textsf{tm}}_{\bullet}
field classifies the meta-space component of a given element; the

reflect
and
reify
fields generalize the familiar operations of
normalization by evaluation, subject to Sterling and Angiuli’s stabilization
yoga
[
33
]
. We finally define both
{\textsf{tp}}^{*}
and
{{\textsf{tm}}^{*}}
using
strict gluing to achieve the correct boundary:

{\textsf{tp}}^{*}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ltimes{\textsf{tp}}_{\bullet}\,A

{{\textsf{tm}}^{*}}\,A=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a:{{\textsf{tm}}}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ltimes\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{ungl}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{ungl}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{ungl}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{ungl}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}.{\textsf{tm}}_{\bullet}\,a

{notation}

Henceforth we will simply write
A\hskip 0.43057pt{\rotatebox{45.0}{\hbox{\rule{1.29167pt}{1.29167pt}}}}\hskip 0.43057pt{\textsf{fld}}
rather than

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{ungl}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{ungl}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{ungl}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{ungl}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}.{\textsf{fld}}
to access a field of the closed component of

A
.

We must also define
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}^{*}:\Omega_{\textit{dec}}
for each
p\in\mathbb{P}
subject
to the condition that
\mathsf{obj}
implies
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}^{*}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
. As there is no
normalization data associated with these propositions, we define
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}^{*}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\displaystyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\textstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}{\mathopen{}{\langle}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}\mathclose{}{\rangle}}
which clearly satisfies the boundary condition.
It remains to show that
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{tp}}^{*},{{\textsf{tm}}^{*}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{tp}}^{*},{{\textsf{tm}}^{*}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{tp}}^{*},{{\textsf{tm}}^{*}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{tp}}^{*},{{\textsf{tm}}^{*}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
are closed under all the connectives of
TT
P
. We show
two representative cases: extension types and the universe.

Extension types

We begin with extension types. Fixing
A:{\textsf{tp}}^{*}
,
p:\mathbb{P}
, and

Remark 13

Stabilized neutrals are crucial to the definition of

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textsf{ext}}^{*}_{p}\,A\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\textsf{ext}}^{*}_{p}\,A\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textsf{ext}}^{*}_{p}\,A\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{ext}}^{*}_{p}\,A\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}\hskip 0.43057pt{\rotatebox{45.0}{\hbox{\rule{1.29167pt}{1.29167pt}}}}\hskip 0.43057pt{\textsf{reflect}}
above: without them, we could not
ensure that reflecting
{\color[rgb]{0.5273,0.0039,0.0664}\textbf{{out}}}_{p}\,A\hskip 0.43057pt{\rotatebox{45.0}{\hbox{\rule{1.29167pt}{1.29167pt}}}}\hskip 0.43057pt{\textsf{code}}\,e
lies within the
specified subtype of
A\hskip 0.43057pt{\rotatebox{45.0}{\hbox{\rule{1.29167pt}{1.29167pt}}}}\hskip 0.43057pt{\textsf{tm}}_{\bullet}
.

Having defined
{\textsf{ext}}^{*}_{p}
in this manner, the definition of
{\textsf{in}}^{*}_{p}
is straightforward:

{\textsf{in}}^{*}_{p}\,A\,a\,x=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{\textsf{in}}_{p}\,A\,a\,x$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{ungl}\,x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{\textsf{in}}_{p}\,A\,a\,x$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{ungl}\,x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{\textsf{in}}_{p}\,A\,a\,x$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{ungl}\,x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{\textsf{in}}_{p}\,A\,a\,x$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{ungl}\,x$}\mathclose{}{]}}

We leave the routine verification of the various boundary conditions to the reader; nearly all of
them follow immediately from the properties of strict gluing.

The universe

We now turn to the construction of the universe in the normalization model; it is here that the
complexity of unstable neutrals becomes evident. Once again the boundary conditions on
{\textsf{uni}}^{*}

fully constrain part of its definition:

{\textsf{uni}}^{*}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{\textsf{uni}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\hbox{\pagecolor{RegalBlue!10}$\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textsf{tp}}_{\bullet}\,{\textsf{uni}}$}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{\textsf{uni}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\hbox{\pagecolor{RegalBlue!10}$\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textsf{tp}}_{\bullet}\,{\textsf{uni}}$}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{\textsf{uni}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hbox{\pagecolor{RegalBlue!10}$\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textsf{tp}}_{\bullet}\,{\textsf{uni}}$}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{obj}\hookrightarrow{\textsf{uni}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\hbox{\pagecolor{RegalBlue!10}$\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.25,0.25}?}:{\textsf{tp}}_{\bullet}\,{\textsf{uni}}$}$}\mathclose{}{]}}

{\textsf{uni}}^{*}
is complex and we present its definition in

Finally, we must show that
{\textsf{uni}}^{*}
is closed under all small type formers and that
{{\textsf{el}}^{*}}

preserves them. This flows from the cumulativity of the universes in
\bm{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textsf{{G}}}}
; to close

{\textsf{uni}}^{*}
under
e.g
.
products, we essentially ‘redo’ the construction of products within
{\textsf{tp}}^{*}

by altering its predicate to be valued in
\mathcal{U}_{0}
rather than
\mathcal{U}_{1}
.

The normalization structure on the universe.

6.7
The normalization algorithm

Having constructed the normalization model in
\bm{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textsf{{G}}}}
, we now extract the actual normalization
algorithm using an argument based on those presented by Fiore
[
14
]
and
Sterling
[
31
]
. Despite the differences in the normalization models, this portion of
the proof closely follows Sterling’s argument.

The unit map

Theorem 6.6

Viewed as objects of
\bm{\mathcal{S}}_{{\textsf{{G}}}}
, the following are levelwise decidable:

{\textsf{nftp}}\qquad\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times{{\textsf{nf}}}\,A\qquad\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A:{\textsf{tp}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi:\Omega_{\textit{dec}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi:\Omega_{\textit{dec}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi:\Omega_{\textit{dec}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi:\Omega_{\textit{dec}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times{{\textsf{ne}}}_{\phi}{A}

and

:

Corollary 1

Definitional equality of types and terms in
TT
P
is decidable,
and type constructors in
TT
P
are definitionally injective.

7
Related work

As discussed in
Section

1
, proof assistants already have support for
various means of controlling the unfolding of definitions; we classify these
mechanisms as either
library-level
or
language-level
.

Library-level features

Various library-level idioms for abstract definitions are used in practice such
as
SSReflect
’s
lock
idiom. While such approaches are flexible
and compatible with existing proof assistants, they are often cumbersome in
practice. For instance,
lock
relies on various tactics with subtle
behavior, which makes it difficult to use
lock
ing idioms in pure
Gallina code.

Language-level features

Many proof assistants include a feature like Agda’s
abstract
blocks which marks a
definition as completely opaque to the remainder of the development. In

Remark

1
, we explained how to recover Agda’s
abstract
definitions
using controlled unfolding. Moreover, as controlled unfolding does not require a user to decide
up front whether a definition can be unfolded, it gives a more realistic and flexible discipline
for abstraction in a proof assistant.
In practice, however,
abstract
is often used for performance reasons instead of merely for
controlling abstraction; unfolding large or complex definitions can significantly slow down type
checking and unification. While we have not discussed performance considerations for controlled
unfolding, the same optimizations apply directly to our mechanism for definitions that are never
unfolded.
In total, controlled unfolding strictly generalizes Agda’s
abstract
blocks.

Translucent ascription in module systems

Thus far we have focused on proof assistants, but similar considerations arise
for ML-style module
systems
[
26
,
22
,
12
,
34
]
.
Interestingly, the default opacity for definitions in module systems is the
same as in controlled unfolding and opposite to proof assistants: types are
abstract unless marked otherwise.
The treatment of translucent type declarations in module
systems
[
22
]
relies on
singleton
kinds

[
4
,
35
]
, which are the special case of
extension types whose boundary proposition is
\top
.
Generalizing from compiletime kinds to mixed compiletime–runtime
module
signatures
, Sterling and Harper have pointed out that transparent ascriptions
are most appropriately handled by means of an extension type whose boundary
proposition represents the compiletime phase
itself
[
34
]
. Thus the translucency of compiletime
module components can be thought of as a
particular
controlled unfolding
policy in the sense of this paper.

8
Conclusions and future work

We have proposed
controlled unfolding
, a new mechanism for interpolating
between transparent and opaque definitions in proof assistants. We have
demonstrated its practical applicability by extending
cool
tt
with
controlled unfolding primitives; we have also proved its theoretical soundness
by constructing an elaboration algorithm to a core calculus whose normalization
we establish using a novel constructive synthetic Tait computability argument.

In the future, we hope to see controlled unfolding integrated into more proof
assistants and to further explore its applications for large-scale organization
of mechanized mathematics. In the context of our
cool
tt
implementation, we
have also already begun to experiment with potential extensions, including one
that allows a
subterm
to be declared locally abstract and then unfolded
later on as-needed — a more flexible alternative to Coq’s
\texttt{abstract}~t

tactical. As we mentioned in
Remark

1
, we also are
interested in facilities to limit the scope in which it is possible to unfold a
given definition.

The LF signature for
TT
P

We present the nonstandard portion of the signature of
TT
